What is the difference between a virtual machine and a container?
A virtual machine (VM) and a container are both types of virtualization technology that allow multiple operating systems to run on a single physical machine. However, there are some key differences between the two:
1. Virtual machine: A virtual machine is a software-based emulation of a physical computer that runs its own operating system and applications. It provides a complete virtual environment for running multiple operating systems and applications on a single physical machine.
2. Container: A container is a lightweight virtualization technology that packages an application and its dependencies into a single unit.
